ParlaMint-DK_20201113111750	Thank you. This is a case that has evolved from one horror scenario to another. Back in June, we found out that the first mink crew was infected with corona. Then perhaps we thought that was what we were worried about, but thought that the situation was under control. But when we returned from vacation in August, the infection continued. So, from 12 October, when there were 76 infected herds, on 30 October 182 herds were infected. And today, 14 days later, we have 255 infected herds. It's a real explosion, and it's deeply, deeply disturbing. It is also deeply disturbing that the infection continues. Now the mink is being killed throughout the country, but right now they are being infected with a rate of five new herds a day. I guess a crew is about 15,000, and that's the number times 5, that's a lot of animals every day. At the same time, there has also been a development in relation to our knowledge in the field, where it can be said that one horror scenario has also replaced the other, so first we find out that mink can get corona, so that mink herds can infect each other for inscrutable reasons, even if they are many kilometres apart; then we find out that mink can infect people; we find out that the mink virus, or the covid-19 virus, mutates in mink; we find out that mutations can even be created that can harm our vaccine work. It's one horror scenario after another. And this knowledge comes out gradually, just as the infection explodes in the crews. The latest figures are 750 dead in Denmark, and currently almost 57,000 are infected with all that it involves on the basis of covid-19. The crisis has drawn great resources from our health system, and it has meant great changes in our everyday lives and the framework for our lives and opportunities to interact and go to work. Many industries and very many people, including some of the most vulnerable groups in our society, have been hit hard. This is a crisis that we must go through together as a society, and we must, of course, do everything we can to stop the spread of infection. I will not spend time on a long-term review of the health authorities' assessment of the risk of coronasmite in mink; this has already been discussed. However, when, on the basis of the government's decision, which has led to this bill, the State Serum Institute concludes that continuing mink breeding during an ongoing covid-19 epidemic poses a significant risk to public health, including the possibility of preventing covid-19 with vaccines, it is indeed an assessment that we, as a government, need to act on and act quickly on. It is, as you know, decided to kill all mink including breeding animals in Denmark. It is a very comprehensive and logistically challenging task which must be solved in the shortest possible time. The demand for the scale of this effort has recently increased considerably. Because, as everyone knows, the spread of covid-19 both in minks and in society has increased dramatically over the past month and, as Mrs Zenia Stampe also mentioned up here, the infection continues to spread. At mink farms at the moment there are 455 farms infected within the zones and 42 farms infected outside the zones. So we simply need to reduce the risk of further spread of infection to people from mink, and we need to eliminate the risk of developing new mutations which may mean that future vaccines will be ineffective. It is simply a question of public health. It is important that we respond quickly and take the necessary measures to maintain epidemic control in Denmark and minimise the risk that new virus mutations in mink spread among humans and, at worst, reduce the future covid-19 vaccines. The aim of the bill here is therefore overall to prevent the spread of covid-19 in mink in order to take account of the significant risk to our health, and at the same time the bill aims to establish the legal basis for requiring the killing of all minks at the Danish mink farms, i.e.. also the healthy mink outside the established risk zones. Finally, the aim of the proposal is also to provide a legal basis for ensuring that the affected mink growers who have inadvertently come into this unhappy situation can be provided with the necessary and fair and proper compensation and compensation for their losses. I would like to take this opportunity here in the Danish Parliament to apologise once again to the Danish mink growers that we have not been able to communicate clearly, that there is currently no legal basis for requiring the killing of healthy mink herds outside the risk zones. However, I would also like to say a great deal of thanks to the mink farmers and the Food and Agriculture Authority and all the authorities and people involved in the work out there now to quickly dispose of the many thousands of minks in a safe and sound way. It is an effort that is being resolved on behalf of Denmark and public health, and for that I should like to thank you very much. In view of the gravity of the situation, the killing of mink should be carried out quickly, and therefore, with this bill, as has been mentioned, a legal basis is being created to pay a tempo bonus to mink growers who are making a quick and effective effort to put their mink down. A lot of people do. As mentioned in the draft law, the legal basis for requiring the killing of healthy mink is also established and, in addition to the legal basis for killing healthy mink outside the risk zones, a prohibition on mink teams up to and including 31 December 2021 is introduced. The government considers it necessary that we stop mink production in Denmark throughout 2021, as continuing mink breeding during an ongoing covid-19 epidemic according to SSI • which I have mentioned involves a significant risk to public health. The draft law also establishes, as I said, the power to provide compensation and compensation to mink farmers. All mink growers will receive full compensation for the loss resulting from the killing of their mink and for the loss resulting from the ban on reinstating mink in 2021. And we introduce into the draft law a similar provision to that laid down in the law on the keeping of animals, which states that mink farmers are entitled to direct compensation for the mink killed, which corresponds to the value of the animals, and compensation to cover the resulting operating losses. The draft law then proposes the introduction of an authorisation to lay down detailed rules on compensation and compensation as a result of the temporary ban on the holding of mink introduced by the proposal. A compensation scheme will be introduced for Danish mink growers, which takes account of the fact that the killing of all Danish mink and the ban on reinstatement put the industry in a very special situation. With the decision to kill mink, we are in a situation where mink farmers will not be able to deploy new mink in 2021 and thus continue their activities. As I have said, direct compensation is granted for the minks killed and for the losses resulting therefrom. Furthermore, the government is proposing that mink growers can choose between two possible models when it comes to compensation. The first model will ensure that mink growers who want it can get out of the industry properly and obtain full compensation for the loss of their business. According to the second model, it must be possible for growers wishing to remain in the mink breeding industry to put their production into hibernation until 31 December 2021 and until it may again be possible to operate mink breeding in Denmark. In addition, as mentioned above, a bonus scheme has been created which means that mink growers receive 20 DKK. in tempobonus pr. mink by a quick killing. In addition to this, growers in North Jutland, where there have been special requests for rapid killing, receive a bonus of DKK 10. per mink. It is important for me to stress that, if it is deemed to be an expropriation in the measurement of compensation for the individual mink, there is, of course, a claim for full compensation. It follows directly from the Constitution. It will be the case that it is based on a specific assessment on a case-by-case basis as to whether it is an expropriation. The assessment depends on a number of factors, including whether the person concerned is carrying out mink breeding as the main occupation, but the government is working on a model in which, in any case, whether it is legally expropriation or not, compensation is granted according to exactly the same principles and on exactly the same conditions. With the bill, we are establishing an authorisation to set up a special mink taksation commission to determine the compensation for each mink grower, and the compensation will be subject to appeal to an enforcement commission. I have already indicated that I and the government will be tabling amendments to the draft law, and I also expect the amendments to reflect the agreement on compensation and the bill here that is being negotiated during these hours. As I said at the outset, it is important that we respond quickly and take the necessary measures to maintain epidemic control in Denmark and minimise the risk of the spread of covid-19 for public health reasons. It is a threat to health throughout our society, and that is what the government is primarily concerned with. Due to the seriousness of the situation, I would like to ask the Danish Parliament for a speedy examination of the bill. It is therefore crucial to have the bill dealt with quickly, also so that Danish mink growers can have clarity about their compensation situation. In those words, I shall look forward to the committee's examination of the draft law, and I would like to thank you once again for the debate.

ParlaMint-DK_20201113141100	Sir. Troels Ravn, the Social Democratic Party.
ParlaMint-DK_20201113141120	Thank you for the floor. In the social democracy we believe it is important to reduce the amount of PVC and phthalates. It is important for Denmark's environment, the health of the Danes and for our future generations. The draft law we are discussing here today aims to reintroduce the PVC tax in order to reduce the amount of PVC and phthalates in Denmark. PVC and phthalates are a plastic material and vinyl is used for pipes, gutters, doors, windows, vinyl floors and carpeting and rainwear. It is part of the government's financial law agreement for 2020 and a levy on PVC and phthalates is necessary because its use is not regulated in any other context because the disposal of PVC has negative environmental and health consequences. PVC contains chlorine which, by incineration, contributes to a significant increase in residues and residues are harmful to our nature, and these residues must also be deposited as hazardous waste. Firstly, we do not have the capacity for this in Denmark, and secondly, it will be to pass our environmental problems on to future generations. To this end phthalates are a problem because some of them are endocrine disruptors and may adversely affect our reproductive capacity. At a time when there are more people seeking fertility treatment to have children, it is particularly important that we do what we can to counteract some of the things that we know can cause the involuntary infertility among the Danes. It is against this background that we want to reintroduce the PVC tax, which the former VLAK government otherwise abolished. It is for our environment, for our health and for our children and grandchildren. It is proposed that the tax base be limited to products where it is estimated that the quantity of soft PVC and phthalates can be measured as a standard rate according to the average content of the product of soft PVC and phthalates and where the tax will have the desired environmental impact. The administrative costs of an inventory for all products containing soft PVC with and without phthalates can be considerable compared to the value of the product and the amount of a charge. Finally, the fight against harmful chemistry in our everyday lives is important for social democracy. We want a green transformation of Denmark, where we fight against the deterioration of Denmark's environment and the health of the Danes. That is why the Social Democratic Party supports the proposal. Thank you.

Is it the case that children's families should say to their children: now your rain clothes have been taxed, so we should not bring that to the day care center today? Or are we going to have bamboo gutters, or what are we supposed to do with this? Today, people organize their papers in ring binders, but don't people need ring binders, so the papers just lie in piles around? Of course, you can do that, and there are probably some who do it in advance, but it is a wasted proposal to put taxes on all things that people use. Should ring band go and turn 1 kroner. More expensive, it's probably not the case that you don't buy them, or should the rainwear be 5 or 10 kroner. More expensive, I think, you send your children to the children's institutions with it anyway. The reason why we removed it together with the previous government was that what is known as a reorganisation committee, that is to say a committee to look at how to simplify the tax system, remove some hacked taxes, and it came to the conclusion that this PVC tax had virtually no effect on health or on the environment, but on the other hand it caused a lot of administrative trouble for companies. It was said quite clearly that the administrative costs do not match the revenue and the lack of environmental effects, and the reason why they did not do so is that this is largely regulated by the European Union. It is quite reasonable, therefore, that the substances which may be in PVC, which are most harmful, are simply banned, so that the challenge lies not in the substances that remain in relation to health. Then there can really be some challenges in terms of recycling, but is it a tax that should be dealt with? I do not believe that, because it is, in relation to what you collect and what you do in connection with incineration plants and elsewhere, but it is not the tax that saves it. As far as I know, there are some 370 companies registered in this area, and the revenues are in 2021 alone of 15 million. kr., quite limited, and in lasting effect it is as low as 5 million. kr. You get almost no money into this, but you bother a lot of companies and make it more expensive for the Danes. This does not make much sense, and perhaps it is also very well reflected in the fact that Denmark, as I know, is the only country in the whole of the European Union that has a levy on PVC and PVC films. If it were to be an extremely good tool for reducing the amount of PVC, it should be strange that there was not just one of the other EU countries that thought it was a good idea to do so. The reason they haven't done it is because it's my guess that it's actually well regulated by EU law, so that the most dangerous substances shouldn't be here. So I think it is a foolish suggestion that the Danes should now pay tax on everything from shower curtains to curtains, to gutters, to tape, to rain clothes and all the other crazy things that are in this proposal. I haven't even mentioned all of them. So the Danish People's Party cannot support this proposal.

ParlaMint-DK_20201113144801	The debate is open and the first to speak is from the Social Democratic Party. It's sir. Troels Ravn.
ParlaMint-DK_20201113144819	Thank you for the floor. The draft law we are discussing today is of a technical nature, but it is nevertheless an important proposal because it ensures some crucial changes and clarifications to the existing legislation, so that, among other things, we can do something about it. ensure better use of resources, better quality of law and respond to business demands. The bill consists of five parts. First of all, the employee stock scheme was adopted almost two years ago, but we have been waiting for EU approval before its final entry into force. It is now available, but it requires some minor adjustments that we want to implement with the legislative proposal. This concerns, among other things: that the main shareholders, listed companies and undertakings which have not repaid unlawful aid are excluded from the scheme. In addition, undertakings are required to report grants of more than 0,5 million. EUR to be published in the EU State Aid Register. Secondly, we are proposing changes to the pension tax rules, which means that misreports to pension schemes can be corrected by the tax administration as long as this is done within 30 days. We are also proposing further simplifications of the rules to facilitate the handling of cases and to save resources. That means, among other things, that... technical amendments to the pension tax and withholding tax law. In addition, we ensure a time-limited option to take out unlisted shares placed in age savings. This responds to the need for more relaxed transitional rules towards the forthcoming change in the FSA's pool regulation. After that, it will no longer be possible to take out unlisted shares placed in age savings. Thirdly, we are proposing a new reporting obligation for financial undertakings to ensure, for example, that: bonus payments to customers are taxed correctly. Fourthly, in order to ensure better legal quality, a need has been identified for clarification of the rules for calculating the transport allowance for air and ferry transport. It is therefore laid down directly in the law of equations rather than in a decree. In addition, the term religions is changed to religious communities in the rules on deductions for contributions to non-profit-making and non-profit-making associations etc. Finally, the draft law clarifies and clarifies the legal situation in relation to the use of arbitration in the international convention to avoid double taxation. This shall be done by withdrawing the reservation to use arbitration only in cases where the chairperson of the arbitration panel is a judge and Denmark may publish a summary of the panel's decisions. There have been objections to the international law of the Danish reservations, which is why we propose that they be withdrawn. These were the five elements of the proposal. With the overall legislative proposal, we can therefore make necessary and optimizing adjustments to existing legislation in this area. Of course, the social democracy supports the proposal. Thank you for the floor.

ParlaMint-DK_20201113150916	Thank you very much for that, President, and may I not start by thanking you for the positive reception of the bill. In my view, however, all the amendments have, of course, been well justified. With a comprehensive proposal, it is clear that it can be difficult, of course, to comment on all the amendments that are in the draft law, so I hope we can also take some of the issues that have been more of a technical nature in committee. In general, however, there are, of course, both different changes and thus different objectives of the changes. In some respects, the draft law contributes to more flexible rules and thus also to resource savings by users. This applies in particular to the proposal for mispayment of pensions in relation to the fact that in future they will have to be corrected without the approval of the tax administration, if only within 30 days. The bill will also contribute to correct tax payments. A new reporting obligation for financial undertakings must ensure, for example, that: bonus payments to customers are taxed. And then the bill will, as Mr. Ole Birk Olesen just mentioned, also contributing to a smooth phasing-in of the forthcoming ban by the FSA against the placement of unlisted shares in age savings, which should prevent tax thinking. Finally, the draft law helps to clarify and clarify the legal situation in a number of areas, including: in relation to the use of arbitration in the International Convention to avoid double taxation and in relation to the rules on transport deductions for ferry and air transport. I would like to make a few comments on two of the amendments to the draft law. The first is the adjustment of the employee stock scheme for new smaller enterprises, so that the scheme can now be put into effect with the Commission's approval here per year. 1 January 2021. The scheme was adopted by the Danish Parliament back in 2019, but since the scheme involves State aid of a nature which has to be approved by the European Commission, the scheme has not yet been put into effect. Now the approval is available, but it does require a few minor adjustments, among other things. the system is cut off in relation to the main shareholders and to listed companies. It is considered that there are relatively limited changes compared to the scheme previously adopted. It has been a long process of approval and I think it is important that the rules are now, of course, allowed to work. That is why it is my view that only the amendments which the Commission has made as a condition for its approval should be made in this respect. If we make other adjustments to the system, it will all have to go through a new process of approval in the Commission, before it can then also enter into force. The second amendment to which I would like to make a few comments is the proposal for a lax transitional rule for pension savers affected by the forthcoming ban on the placement of unlisted shares in old-age savings by the FSA. In the previous government agreement on business and entrepreneurial initiatives of November 2017, it was agreed to relax the placement rules for pension funds. However, this relaxation was never implemented, including in particular: because the Ministry of Taxation drew attention to the possibility that a trader could achieve a significantly lower tax burden on his work by placing shares in his company in an age savings. That is what the radical rapporteur explained most prominently. This tax-thinking option, if you like, will only increase even further if the agreed reductions are implemented. They were not implemented under the former government, although they were agreed, because the Treasury warned against exactly this. For the government, it is crucial to eliminate opportunities for tax thinking so that we ensure that everyone contributes their fair share to the tasks we have in developing our welfare society, for example. That is why the FSA is introducing a ban that will effectively prevent this. In addition to the ban imposed by the FSA, we are proposing, at the same time, with this draft law, a lenient transitional rule which indicates that we are doing things in a fair way when we change the rules. The transitional rule ensures that no one is forced to sell their shares as a result of the changes. It will thus be possible for the persons concerned to withdraw the unlisted shares from the scheme without paying the usual tax. It is also possible, under the applicable law, to buy the shares for free funds so that the age savings are maintained or to sell the unlisted shares to a third person. So it is a matter of relaxation here, and it is a question of ensuring that a hole is closed. This is therefore a fair arrangement and a relaxation of the current rules. In those words, I would like to thank you for the debate and, of course, look forward to discussing this proposal in the Committee on Taxation. And, of course, I would like to say now that the government is prepared for us to divide up the proposal. But it is important for me to know the reasons for this change. As I said, it is the case that the previous government tried, but it was warned against, it was not changed. Now the hole that was warned against is being closed and the tax rules are being relaxed so that you can withdraw the shares from this. That is the point of the proposal.

Since its introduction in 1959, the ceiling has been raised several times, but so far there has been a broad consensus here too that there should be a ceiling. The deduction ceiling does not prevent larger amounts, if you like, so to speak, from being donated, but there is only a limit on how much deduction can be made for donations. That is why you can get a deduction and then there is the inappropriate thing in that state per year. automatic pays 25%. of the donations that citizens would like to make. ParlaMint-DK_20201113153407	Thank you. Unfortunately, the SF rapporteur could not be here, but I have promised to read his speech. The SF cannot support the proposal. We believe, as a starting point, that it is sympathetic for citizens to donate money for charitable purposes. However, we think that the provisional limit of 16.600 DKK. in tax credit is reasonable, not least because the normal employee in Denmark can hardly afford to donate a higher amount per year. Nor is it possible to donate more money than that amount. You just can't deduct the total amount of donations from tax as soon as it exceeds this limit. If we remove the ceiling and allow people to withdraw all donations from their taxes, we risk a distortion of charity. The scheme may result in a minority being given control over the projects to be financed. To this end, it is important to note what a charitable purpose entails. On tax.dk is reported that it is organisations that range from humanitarian purposes to cultural houses, museums and sports associations. We therefore run the risk of potential tax dollars being distributed by private citizens to local, specific initiatives that only benefit a very narrow and not vulnerable group. Therefore, should it be mentioned to regulate the ceiling on donations for charitable purposes, we in the SF propose to create restrictions on which organisations may be within the raised limit. Then it is also important to find out how this scheme is to be financed, because we completely remove the ceiling for donations to charity, the state will lose essential tax dollars that could be spent on the welfare of all citizens.

ParlaMint-DK_20201113154019	Thank you. The Liberal Alliance supports the motion for a resolution. We think it's really good. And I would like to explain why, in our view, and contrary to what Mr Enhedslisten's rapporteur said, it is good that people out there are allowed to spend their own money for charity rather than the money being collected by the Treasury and then used for benevolent purposes. There is, of course, the advantage of allowing people to keep their money, that they then have greater pluralism in the distribution of money for benevolent purposes. So there are many different people with many different perceptions of what is worthy to support and spend money on supporting. However, if you take all the money into the treasury, in principle the decision-making majority decides on the money every time. Let's say there are 1,000 people out there, and in principle there are 1,000 different options for who benefits from that money. But if these 1,000 people's money comes into the treasury, you decide on the first thousandth of them, and it is the majority that decides. Then you decide on the second thousandth of them... it's certainly the majority again who decides. Then you decide on the third thousandth of them and again it is the majority who decides. And so it is every time So, it is the majority who decides every time, and the majority can be quite the same from time to time, and thus there will be less pluralism in society. And that is what is the great untruth of hardcore socialism, in other words, in relation to what you want in this society. It is often claimed that one is on the side of pluralist society, the side of tolerant society and the side of diverse society, but the pluralistic, diverse and tolerant society finds it difficult to finance itself when it is always the state and the majority who decide who should have money. Because then only those who are good friends with the state get paid. And that's what you see. also in the distribution of money to cultural life in Denmark. It's about the same people who get the money all the time. If the money had remained out with the citizens and with the companies, perhaps some more people would benefit from the money, instead of always being able to indulge themselves with the state and with the majority who are getting the money. We shall vote in favour of the motion for a resolution.

ParlaMint-DK_20201113154807	Thank you for that, President. Today we are dealing with a motion for a resolution by the Socialist People's Party on regulating the price at which tobacco products may be sold in retail trade. SF proposes to prohibit tobacco producers from selling tobacco products with financial losses and to set a date when tobacco products can no longer be sold at an old price, in the context of the forthcoming tax increase, which will therefore come here in 2022. With the proposal, the SF wants to ensure compliance with the political intentions that tobacco products should be more expensive, with the aim that there is, of course, broad agreement here in the Folketing, namely to reduce the number of smokers, including in particular to prevent children and young people from starting to smoke at all. I would like to start by saying that the government agrees with the intentions behind the resolution, and that is why the government, as the authors of the motion for a resolution know very well, is already in full swing to pave the way for the tobacco tax increases to become a reality. In the 2020 Finance Act, we increased tobacco taxes because, as a government, we want fewer people to start smoking, including children and young people in particular. If, several months after the increase, you can still find cigarettes at an old price, then you have to say that it goes against the objective that we broadly agree in the Folketing. What we are seeing is that tobacco producers are now working directly against the purpose of the increase. They have dumped prices and ordered stamp marks with a value which means that they simply lose money selling their cigarettes right now. As a tax minister, I have to say that I think it is both gross but also quite distasteful that the purpose of the legislation, which comes from even a very broad majority in this Parliament, is not respected. The government has therefore recently launched a plan of action with seven concrete actions. Three of the measures concern tobacco taxes, which must be increased so that it is reflected in tobacco prices, and at the same time what is called subsidiary nicotine products must be taxed, so that the consumption of tobacco products by the Danes is not simply moved to, if you like, other unhealthy products. Firstly, as I have said, we have already approved the increase in the tobacco tax by another DKK 5. It shall take place on 1 January 2022. Secondly, the government has contacted the European Commission to put pressure on Member States to introduce minimum prices for tobacco. Thirdly, we will also look at the possibility of introducing tax on new products, including nicotine products without tobacco, as we have already done with regard to e-cigarettes. Two other measures of the seven measures taken by the government concern the requirements for tobacco trade, which must be tightened up to ensure that, of course, the tax increases will also have that effect without the effect being shifted or that trade, particularly with cigarettes, will move across the border. Firstly, we want to tighten the rules for and against the hoarding of tobacco companies, so that tobacco producers can buy at most 2%. additional stamp marks with old tax prior to the increase, and the next increase will therefore come on 1 January 2022. Secondly, we want to restrict the sale of tobacco with old stamp marks, so that they can only be sold for up to three months after the next tax increase. It is also called clean market, if you like, and is one of the proposals which are also called for in the resolution that we are debating here today. In addition, two proposals of the seven concern control of the trade in tobacco products, which must be strengthened in order to ensure that tobacco producers and tobacco distributors comply with the rules. It has to have noticeable consequences to break these rules. First of all, and we are already doing so together in our so-called comprehension, we want to provide more resources to control the trade of goods subject to excise duties. This also applies to tobacco, in connection with the recruitment of 250 new control officers here in 2021. Secondly, we are going to look into the possibility of imposing fines on tobacco producers and, moreover, tobacco dealers in order to counteract the sale of tobacco with old stamp marks. With this action plan, the government will put forward a legislative proposal as soon as possible which will implement the action plan, so that hopefully what we have seen will be put an end to. I think it is gross and I think it is distasteful, and I can only see it as having one purpose: to create greater dependence on tobacco, especially among young people. And that goes directly against the intentions of a broad majority in the Folketing. The government supports the intentions behind the resolution and, as I have just said, we have already put forward a plan of action. As I said, this also includes a proposal on clean market; we have addressed the European Commission on the question of minimum prices. As I have said, we have a number of other proposals to help put an end to this and to the behaviour we have seen here recently. I think it's distasteful. I don't think it belongs anywhere. And I think we have a common interest in dealing with it. But the resolution as it stands here cannot be supported by the government.

And, of course, we expect the tobacco industry to play by the rules as well. We know that the vast majority of smokers begin while they are very young. We know that every day there are approx. 40 young people who start smoking, and it's incredibly hard to quit once you've started. ParlaMint-DK_20201113161009	Thank you. The background to the proposal is actually quite back when a significant increase in the tax on cigarettes was made, which the Danish People's Party did not vote through. The problem then arises in that there are some who have been allowed to get very many stamps or tax marks, or whatever it is called, handed over by the tax minister's official work. And it is clear that if there is one company that has cigarettes for a cheap tax, while the others actually follow the law and the intention behind it and raise prices, then market shares will naturally begin to move. Because if you're a smoker, you're probably quite naturally buying the cheap cigarettes as long as you can. And the consequence, as has been seen out there, has been that those who had the expensive tax marks reduced the price in order to keep their market shares still in check, so they sold with losses. Somehow one cannot imagine them in that they tried to play by the rules from the start, but that they were out-competeed by those who had the old tax marks and got a competitive advantage, because they had been awarded these tax marks by the Treasury or by the Board of Directors, and then one can really understand the problem. But that is why it is still fair enough that we in the Folketing are trying to discuss how to overcome it. And it gets more forward-looking in terms of how to solve it. The proposal here is that a minimum political price should be set, and I do not think that is a good idea, and I do not think it is a viable one either. I think it is a good thing that we are setting a level of taxation, but that we are actually setting what the price level should be, I do not think it is a good idea. But I also believe that in the long term the problem will automatically solve itself, at least what is there right now, because there are no companies that can run with deficits for a very long time, yes, perhaps Shell can do so, but otherwise there are not many who can run with deficits for a longer period. So I think it equals itself. But you can also try to reflect on whether, if you transfer it to other industries, then actually makes sense. You can, for example. take Bilka, who every Friday sells candy at prices below the tax level. There you might also say: 'Well, is it so against the political intention that you should take a certain price for candy, because it is something that fattens'. And it is, but they do it to get people into the store and also buy all sorts of other things. And the rest of the week, it's not so cheap. And you could say that we, for example. Right now, we are selling cars and saying that ordinary black cars, that is, those who drive petrol and diesel, must be more expensive in order to give an incentive to drive in a green car. But if the price does not rise on it, would we say that it is against the political intention and that we should then set the price of a car? I just don't think that's necessarily the best way to do that. So I think that the solution and what you have to learn from this is that you have to put a pretty tough limit on how many of these tax marks or stamp marks or what they are called. And we could also set a clear deadline for when everyone should be on the new tax structure so that this situation is not restored. And if you have such old stamp marks, they don't apply anymore, and if you have products where they are on, then you have to call them back from the stores and maybe also with high penalties if you get into that situation. So I kind of agree with the problem, that it causes a lot of problems out there, because when one company has some old stamp marks that are made, the others are quite pressed, and you can understand that when they actually try to comply with the rules and do what you have to do. But I do not think a minimum price is good enough for us to set politically. But we'd like to take a look at whether you can make some tough restrictions on what you're doing to stop handing out these old stamps, and also look at some cut-off date, which may well be close to when the new cigarette prices will come into effect. Then we have to stop seeing them in the store and otherwise fines and sanctions and other things can be imposed. So we are not in favour of the method, but are in favour of the problem and the objective we want to achieve.

This should always be done on the basis of a concrete and comprehensive assessment of what is best for the individual child. The possibility of depriving a parent who has been convicted of terrorism, the right to association, residence or parental responsibility already exists in the parental responsibility law today. Therefore, we see no reason to extend the presumption rule, which is why we do not support L 15.

ParlaMint-DK_20201113165235	The debate is open. Sir. Daniel Toft Jakobsen, Social Democratic Party.
ParlaMint-DK_20201113165246	Thank you. The draft law here will make it clear that children and young people with disabilities have the right to what is called fair individualisation in day care and in primary school. Basically, it is about giving equal opportunities and avoiding discrimination. Reasonable individual adjustment means that offers and services are adapted so that people with disabilities can make use of the offers and benefit from the benefits as any other person can. When a given adaptation is reasonable will depend on a specific individual assessment, which will examine both the extent to which a given adaptation will make for the children concerned, but also the cost of making the adjustment. When it says in the legislative notes that the bill clarifies the right to fair individual adaptation and thus clarifies this right, it is because there are already some rules on what consideration and what adjustments are to be made today and in the primary school for children and young people with disabilities. These rules do not change with this bill, but the right to fair individual adjustment in day-to-day offers and in primary school is now written directly into the law on the prohibition of discrimination on the grounds of disability. And that's good, among other things. Because of this it will also be possible to appeal to the Equal Treatment Board and, if necessary, to obtain compensation if this right is not respected. The bill translates part of the agreement between the government and KL on the economy of the municipalities by 2020. This is a good proposal, which is basically about equal opportunities for children and young people with disabilities. Of course, the social democracy supports the proposal.

In SF we believe that in some cases this may be a disproportionate burden for individual institutions and schools, as the municipalities here interpret the economy very differently. The assessment will therefore benefit from the municipal council's overall budget for primary school or day-off services. We, together with the disability organisations, are also concerned that the draft law does not provide for private schools and day-to-day services. One might find it ironic that we are trying to combat discrimination by discrimination against children and young people, depending on whether they go to public or private day care or schools. ParlaMint-DK_20201113171925	Thank you. The transition to adult life for young people with disabilities can be very difficult, because when a person with disabilities turns 18, there are many things that change. It is simply a number of rules and clauses that apply to adults other than those applicable to children. Unfortunately, it is too often that young people with disabilities are left without sufficient help at the age of 18. That's what we're trying to do about this bill. The aim of the proposal is to ensure that municipalities initiate the planning of the transition to adult life for young people with disabilities at the age of 16. This must, of course, be done in dialogue with the young person and his or her parents. Among other things, consideration must be given to: be the need for assistance and support, education, employment and dependency, housing, social conditions and other relevant matters. The early start of preparation should very much mean that more young people with a physical or mental impairment will have a good and safe transition to adult life. We simply need to be better at creating a good transition from child to adult life for people with disabilities. The draft law translates an initiative from a broad political agreement on the reserve for social, labour and health measures for 2020-2023, an agreement concluded last autumn and the Social Democratic Party of course backs this proposal.

And then we will have to take the discussion further, also on the basis of it, in relation to the specific legislative proposal here.
